Greek Symphony gets the nod on the most coherent recent profile: a dominant AW win five days ago, preceded by a 0.3L second on good ground at Newbury, plus draw 4 in a high-draw-biased Epsom 7f band (~41% of winners) and a useful effective light weight with the 3lb claim. He looks the most likely to run his race again in a wide-open Class 5 with minimal course form across the field.

The main danger is surface translation — his strike rate is built on AW and he is 0-1 on today's going — while Oliver Cole's yard and Ashley Lewis sit well down the six-month ranks and bring no recent J14 momentum. Wise And Wonderful is the chief alternative on the sole genuine 7f good-ground line (beaten only 0.2L in a Class 2 Goodwood handicap under Andrew Balding), but draw 3 is neutral at best, he carries top weight with just two runs, and finishing tenth there leaves pace/trip doubts in a five-runner sprint.

Raintown is the clearest fit: he won this exact Newbury 10f Good to Firm Class 5 test three weeks ago, is 1-2 course-and-distance and 2-5 at the track, and his last three runs are all useful Newbury efforts over 10f-11f. Draw 2 is fine in a neutral-to-mid-biased 10f sample, the going suits (2-5 on good), and he has already shown he can carry top weight here. Decalogue is next best on raw improvement — draw 1, highest mark, and a neck-close third at Newmarket — but he is untried at 10f and has no course record, which keeps him behind the proven course-and-distance winner.

The main danger is not ability but setup: in a five-runner handicap, 10-0 and a cold apprentice jockey leave little margin if Decalogue or We Ride At Dawn (recent 10f winner, well handicapped on 9-7) improve sharply. Grey Fox’s Windsor second is a minor threat if the three prominent types (Raintown, Decalogue, Living In Hope) cut it up, but his 0-8 at the trip and winless jockey/trainer form last fortnight cap his true chance below the front two.

Divot is the most race-fit profile on the evidence, not the shortest price. He has won twice here in five weeks on Good to Firm (10f, Class 6), with strong going (2-4) and course (2-4) records; draw 2 sits in the low-draw third that has won 34% of Yarmouth ~12f races, and his 8-13 from a 65 OR is a meaningful weight relief against Arcturus Flame (75, 9-9) and Zachary Hickes (74, 9-8). The Kempton 12f fifth, beaten only half a length, supports the extra half-furlong. At 7.4 the market implies roughly 13%; a true chance nearer one-in-three makes him the logical selection over a favourite priced for reputation.

The main danger is that those Yarmouth wins came in weaker Class 6 company over ten furlongs, and today’s Class 5 step with Knight/Spencer cold (T14 1-9, J14 0-8) explains the drift from 4.8 to 7.4. Arcturus Flame is the chief threat: top OR, draw 1, James Owen/Cieren Fallon in strong form, and a Goodwood 14f third showing he stays, even if he lacks course/distance proof and arrives off a void with patchy recent figures. Do not equate his 2.2 quote with a ~45% win rate; in a four-runner handicap the likeliest scenario is a tight finish among the top three, with Good Onya Mate…

CursorNicely Curved · 19%
Risk Seven runs without a win — consistent thirds may not convert.
exact pace map with multiple prominent typeswhether Heer's Sadie drift reflects a stable concernlate watering changing effective ground
Nicely Curved is my marginal top pick on true chance, not market rank. She maps well to today: high draw (39% of Yarmouth 8f winners come from the high third), proven 8f form including a neck-third over this trip on soft here, and a close third on good/firm at the course twelve days ago. Lingfield third over 8f good/firm confirms trip suitability; the June Yarmouth interference note offers a plausible excuse for one narrow defeat. At ~5.7 she is not priced like a banker, which fits a winless but repeatedly competitive profile.

The biggest threat is Heer's Sadie: course-and-distance winner (1-6 CD), second over this course, trip, going and class seventeen days ago, also from a helpful draw. Letsbeatsepsis looks overbet at 2.6 — the Windsor win was over 10f, he carries top weight with a 7lb-claiming jockey on a 0-14 hot streak, and the Brighton blowout (even with a stewards’ enquiry) caps enthusiasm. I would not stake this race strongly; the model’s no-qualifier call aligns with a genuinely fragmented Class 6 handicap where no runner clears ~20% true win probability.

Modern Times gets the nod on profile rather than price: he arrives off a fresh 6f win (17 days), carries a workable OR 70 from draw 3, and James Owen’s yard is the hottest trainer representation on the card. That recent trip and momentum matter more here than Ruby’s Profit’s top mark or Sir Garfield’s older Catterick win, both of which look exposed on current form.

The biggest threat is On Edge, whose Good-ground (4-10) and 6f (5-23) records fit today better than almost anyone’s, and Mason’s 1-2 T14 adds confidence despite a 52-day break and no Epsom course form. Angel Gabriel (draw 1, Beckett/Crouch, promising 6f GF run) is the other live danger if the low draw pays on this straight six.

Sunlit Sea looks the most likely winner on fundamentals: a clear 5f Good-to-Firm win at Thirsk six days ago, 1-1 on today’s going, useful draw 4 on a course where draw is secondary, and a 6lb weight edge over Creative Queen and London Is Blue despite a lower mark. Thirsk form reads stronger than a bare 1/9 — she won decisively at the trip and surface the market often overweights in favour of bigger yards.

The main danger is Creative Queen: William Haggas and Cieren Fallon are in strong 6-month form, she was only a length off in a five-runner Ripon 5f handicap on Good, and draw 8 fits Yarmouth’s slight high-draw bias (39% winners). London Is Blue’s short price is not ability proof — 10/11 at Windsor and weak 5f figures make him a poor win proposition — while Rumble Ruby’s Beverley bolt two days ago is offset by top weight and a negative market drift. I would not treat this as a strong betting race despite Sunlit Sea leading the win estimates.

Sorrengail gets the nod on the strongest win on the key test: Ascot 6f Good To Firm Class 3, beating a bigger field than most here, with a narrow Newmarket defeat before that showing he handles the trip and surface. Charlie Appleby’s yard is the hottest trainer stat on the card (7-14), and William Buick is a top-tier booking even if his recent 14-day strike is cooler. Draw 3 sits in the neutral middle band — acceptable on a card where high draws have a modest edge, unlike low-draw rivals.

The main pushback is calibration, not the identity of the pick: 51% is far too bullish for a six-runner Class 2 nursery where four horses arrive off wins and profiles are still forming. Coat Of Red is a serious second — Leicester 6f form and a close Goodwood Class 3 fourth are comparable — but draw 1 works slightly against the Newbury 6f bias and Callum Rodriguez’s recent 1-13 run tempers the Crisford angle. Evenfall’s Billy Loughnane/Boughey link is elite, yet the sole win was at five furlongs; Ferrystream has draw 6 and Oisin Murphy but an opaque Windsor win and the longest gap since running. Nonrem is out of contention on weight, stale form, and a poor Salisbury Class 2 run.

pace map / early speedORs for unrated runnerssectional or timefigure comparison
Dubai Charm gets the nod mainly on task-fit rather than market: draw 13 aligns with Newbury’s 5f high-draw bias (45% of winners), she is fresh off a close third and a win at Yarmmouth over 5f on good to firm, and her going/distance record (1-3 on today’s ground, 1-2 at 5f) is stronger than most rivals. Pat Dobbs and Richard Hughes add solid if not elite strike-rate support, and the step back from six furlongs should suit more than her Ascot Class 1 flop did.

The biggest risk is that Ascot failure under Class 1 conditions — plus several rivals with sharper recent lines (Angels Lane’s Newbury second, Fast Track’s Goodwood second, Elevating’s Sandown win) — means she may need everything to go right from the widest stall; Angels Lane is next best on pure course-and-distance suitability, penalised for draw 2 on this track profile.

true market pricesofficial ratingsearly pace/debutant running plans
National Honour is the clearest form horse: a 2L second at Newmarket (July) over 6f on good ground in a competitive field, with Charlie Appleby’s yard striking at 50% (7-14) and James Doyle a solid booking. That recent run beats Breaking The Habit’s remote third (8L), Gold Key’s distant ninth, and Varzi’s stale 5f York placing from 93 days ago on the wrong trip. The model’s lean is fair on ability and connections, though I trim the probability from 41% to 34% given calibration and setup friction.

The main danger is draw-led rather than price-led: Thirsk 7f strongly favors high stalls (46% of winners), which lifts Work Like Hell from stall 6 with Daniel Tudhope in good recent form — a plausible upset if Tate’s debutant is fit and forward. Varzi is a minor each-way speck on class alone but low draw, layoff, and cold Burke/Jamin numbers cap him; Meccapulco (stall 1, 5lb top weight) and Gold Key look the least likely on draw, weight, and form combined.

World As One looks the most reliable winner on form rather than price. Willie Mullins sends few hurdlers here without purpose, Brian Hayes rides a yard in strong recent form, and this five-year-old has completed every start with no P/F/U — important on a 16.5f Good hurdle. The profile fits: LR24, 75% rtf, three tight defeats (beaten 1L, 0.5L, 2.8L) over 16–18f on good ground. At 5.3 he is not the market leader, which fits a true chance nearer 23% than the ~19% implied.

The main worry is the 2-453 pattern — he keeps finding one or two too good. Chasing Blues is the logical second: a 0.8L second at Wexford over 16f on good is the clearest recent hurdle form in the race, from a top yard, but the Killarney unseating and only three runs leave a jumping/fitness question. Simple Things is overbet at 2.6 on OR/weight alone; Galway (6/20) and 0-6 over today's trip argue his true chance is well below favourite status. Seven Towers has Tramore course form but a winless run of thirds; the rest are debuts, pull-ups, or stale/long-absent profiles with single-digit true chances.

The drift from 3.5 to 9.0 and 45 days since her last run also leave a fitness question the bare figures cannot fully settle.

Return Of The Gods is the most likely winner on form: a proven Class 4 seven-furlong winner (Doncaster), only half a length off the winner at York over this trip, carrying top weight credibly, and Edward Bethell/Daniel Tudhope are in good nick. I would not stake the race at current prices — the favourite is overbet and the win pool looks spread across several live chances rather than dominated by one horse.

Hickory is the most convincing profile on the evidence, not the price. He won an 8f Good To Firm Ascot handicap off a similar mark last month, and on 07-09 at this same July course, trip, going and Class 3 he was sixth of 13 beaten only 0.3L — far stronger than Sterling Knight (third, 3.8L back) from that day. OR 93 keeps him near the top weights with proven 8f form; draw five sits in the neutral middle third where plenty of winners come from, and Jamie Osborne's yard is workable even if not red-hot.

The main challenge is a tactical one, not ability: if he is asked to make or force a strong pace, the high-draw closers — especially progressive Brocklesby Bill (Sandown 8f winner, 2-3 on Good/Firm, strong stall nine in a 46% high-draw band) and Croupier (3-7 at the trip, Robert Havlin riding well) — are the likeliest to capitalise. Storm Star tops the weights on OR 96 and Andrew Balding form, but a 57-day gap and a poor Ascot return cap his true win chance below the favourite hype the market might attach.

Saxony Charms is the most trip-proven runner here: two Curragh miles, third on Good then fifth beaten only 0.1L last time when the bare result understates the effort. Colin Keane is in strong recent form, and at 6.8 the market still treats that Curragh line as modest — I have him at roughly 21%, ahead of Zadawa (~17%), whose two close 10f runs also fit but are heavily docked on draw 12 in a full field. Garden Party is short on name and Moore, not evidence: 291 days off, both runs at 7f, and no proof over 10.5f on Good — I make him ~15%, not a 2.3 chance. Serendipia’s OR 75 and recent Gowran third are positives, but she is stepping up from 8f; Fairylike’s debut draw and pedigree are interesting without displacing the two mile-form horses.

The main risk to Saxony Charms is race shape rather than ability — a lightly raced profile from stall 8, with Zadawa compromised wide and Garden Party or Fairylike potentially setting or forcing a pace from better draws under the same trainer. If the favorite is fully fit after layoff, the win probability drops materially even if the price still overstates him.

Military Leader is my marginal top pick on true ability, not market rank. He has the clearest course-and-distance profile in the field (1-2 at Thirsk, 2-8 at 8f), arrives off a strong Chester second and that decisive Thirsk win in similar company, and he lands on the favoured part of the draw — high stalls have taken 43% of 8f winners here versus 24% low. CursorLaunch Time · 14%
Risk A 19-runner handicap can be stolen by a low-OR hot streaker even when the top-weight looks more logically placed.
pace map / early speedwhether Hughes prioritises Launch Time over Thompson Gunnerexact going vs April Cork form reads
Launch Time is the most coherent win profile on paper: OR 57 with a recent Leopardstown win, a prior Cork 8f fourth (April), Colin Keane in strong card form, and draw 14 in a race where high stalls have taken ~46% of winners over six years—far above low (27%). He is not a short-priced “proof” horse; at 6.2 the market still leaves room for a true chance near 14%, the highest in the field on balance of weight, suitability, and draw.

The main danger is Reponse Finale: the Gowran 1/20 two days ago is real improvement and Cromwell’s yard is live, but OR 49, no Cork/distance wins, mid draw, and a poor Cork 8f run in April argue the 2.6 quote overstates ability—she is the likeliest upset second rather than the best win bet. Wide-field variance and Hughes running two (Launch Time vs Thompson Gunner) add tactical noise; that is why the model’s no-bet stance is understandable even with Launch Time on top.
